But I keep replaying things—every camera angle I didn’t catch, every call I maybe made too late—and it starts to feel like proof.\n\nLike I’m careless. Dangerous. And if something happens while I’m there… part of me thinks I should be punished for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed, without clear evidence, that someone at work was trying to set you up or make you look careless, or that they were trying to harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, people get annoyed at work, sure, but I haven’t really thought someone was setting me up or trying to hurt me. It’s more… I worry about what I might’ve missed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that others have told you isn’t true, even if you couldn’t explain why you believed it, such as thinking you were responsible for something serious happening even if there was no real evidence to support that?\n\nPatient: Yes… that happens. I know people tell me it’s not proof, but I keep going back over what I missed—camera angles, calls I didn’t make fast enough—and it feels like it means I’m careless.\n\nLike if something bad happens on my watch, then… I should be punished for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed, even for a short time, that you were going to be punished for something you did — or didn’t do — even if there was no real reason to think that would happen?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes. Not like I have a clear reason, exactly.\n\nIt’s more that I keep going over every camera angle I missed, or if I made a call too late, and it starts feeling like… evidence. Like I’m careless, or dangerous. And if something goes wrong while I’m on watch, part of me feels like I should be punished for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed, strongly and without clear proof, that something was happening to you that wasn’t actually true—like that you were being watched, followed, or that something bad was about to happen to you, even if others told you it wasn’t real?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not watched or followed.\n\nI do get worried, but it’s more about whether I missed something or did something wrong. Not that someone is after me, really.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly—without clear proof—that wasn’t about being punished or missing something at work, something others might not agree with but felt completely true to you at the time?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so.\n\nIt’s mostly been that one kind of thing—work, what I missed, what I should’ve done. I can’t really think of anything else like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed strongly in something related to your work—like being punished for mistakes or missing something important—even when there was no real proof or official action to support that belief?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s closer to it.\n\nI’ll replay a shift over and over—like a camera angle I didn’t check, or a call I made late—and it starts to feel like proof I’m careless. Dangerous, even. And if something happens while I’m there… part of me feels like I should be punished for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs that others might not share—about things like your health, relationships, or other parts of your life—that you felt were definitely true, even if there wasn’t clear proof?\n\nPatient: Not really… no.\n\nI mean, I worry about things, but I can’t think of anything like that outside of work. Not about my health or relationships or anything.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or… that people are going to get hurt because of me. No one else seems to hear it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that these voices are trying to communicate a specific message or warn you about something that you believe to be true, even though others might not agree with you?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say they’re warning me exactly. It comes through the monitor speakers when it’s quiet, even muted. I know that probably sounds wrong, but in the room, when it’s quiet and the speakers are muted… it feels real. No one else hears it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something to be true, even though others have told you it isn’t, such as thinking someone is in danger because of something you missed?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes I do. What brings you here today?\n\nPatient: I… I’m here because something at work scared me. I work in surveillance, and during quiet stretches I’ve been hearing voices through the monitor speakers, saying I missed something or that people are going to get hurt because of me. But the channels are muted, and… no one else hears it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that others don’t hear, outside of your work environment?\n\nPatient: Not really outside of there. It’s mostly in the surveillance room… through the monitor speakers, when it’s quiet. Even when everything’s muted. That’s the part that scared me.\n\nDoctor: When you're in other quiet places outside of work, like a library or your home at night, have you ever heard voices or sounds that others don't seem to hear?\n\nPatient: No… not really. It’s been in the surveillance room, when it’s quiet. I hear it like it’s coming through the monitor speakers, even when they’re muted. Things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or… that people are going to get hurt because of me. No one else seems to hear it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that these voices are trying to communicate a specific message or warn you about something that you believe to be true, even though others might not agree with you?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say they’re warning me exactly. But it feels like they’re saying I missed something. Like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or that people are going to get hurt because of me. It comes through the monitor speakers when it’s quiet, even muted. No one else hears it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that these voices are trying to tell you something important about events or people, even if others would say it's not true?\n\nPatient: I guess… it feels important when it happens. Like it’s telling me I missed something on the screens, or that someone’s going to get hurt because of me. I know that probably sounds wrong, but in the room, when it’s quiet and the speakers are muted… it feels real. No one else hears it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something to be true, even though others have told you it isn’t, such as thinking someone is in danger because of something you missed?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes I do. I keep going back over every camera angle I might’ve missed, every call I didn’t make fast enough, and it feels like proof that I’m… careless. Dangerous, maybe.\n\nAnd if something went wrong on my watch, part of me feels like I’d deserve whatever happened to me for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that others have told you isn’t true, even if it was hard to explain, like thinking you were responsible for something that wasn’t your fault or that someone was in danger because of you?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I know people say it isn’t all on me. But I keep replaying things—every camera angle I didn’t catch, every call I maybe made too late—and it starts to feel like proof.\n\nLike I’m careless. Dangerous. And if something happens while I’m there… part of me thinks I should be punished for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed, without clear evidence, that someone at work was trying to set you up or make you look careless, or that they were trying to harm you in some way?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, people get annoyed at work, sure, but I haven’t really thought someone was setting me up or trying to hurt me. It’s more… I worry about what I might’ve missed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that others have told you isn’t true, even if you couldn’t explain why you believed it, such as thinking you were responsible for something serious happening even if there was no real evidence to support that?\n\nPatient: Yes… that happens. I know people tell me it’s not proof, but I keep going back over what I missed—camera angles, calls I didn’t make fast enough—and it feels like it means I’m careless.\n\nLike if something bad happens on my watch, then… I should be punished for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed, even for a short time, that you were going to be punished for something you did — or didn’t do — even if there was no real reason to think that would happen?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes. Not like I have a clear reason, exactly.\n\nIt’s more that I keep going over every camera angle I missed, or if I made a call too late, and it starts feeling like… evidence. Like I’m careless, or dangerous. And if something goes wrong while I’m on watch, part of me feels like I should be punished for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed, strongly and without clear proof, that something was happening to you that wasn’t actually true—like that you were being watched, followed, or that something bad was about to happen to you, even if others told you it wasn’t real?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not watched or followed.\n\nI do get worried, but it’s more about whether I missed something or did something wrong. Not that someone is after me, really.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly—without clear proof—that wasn’t about being punished or missing something at work, something others might not agree with but felt completely true to you at the time?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so.\n\nIt’s mostly been that one kind of thing—work, what I missed, what I should’ve done. I can’t really think of anything else like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed strongly in something related to your work—like being punished for mistakes or missing something important—even when there was no real proof or official action to support that belief?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s closer to it.\n\nI’ll replay a shift over and over—like a camera angle I didn’t check, or a call I made late—and it starts to feel like proof I’m careless. Not like I have a clear reason, exactly.\n\nIt’s more that I keep going over every camera angle I missed, or if I made a call too late, and it starts feeling like… evidence. Like I’m careless, or dangerous. And if something goes wrong while I’m on watch, part of me feels like I should be punished for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed, strongly and without clear proof, that something was happening to you that wasn’t actually true—like that you were being watched, followed, or that something bad was about to happen to you, even if others told you it wasn’t real?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not watched or followed.\n\nI do get worried, but it’s more about whether I missed something or did something wrong. Not that someone is after me, really.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly—without clear proof—that wasn’t about being punished or missing something at work, something others might not agree with but felt completely true to you at the time?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so.\n\nIt’s mostly been that one kind of thing—work, what I missed, what I should’ve done. I can’t really think of anything else like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed strongly in something related to your work—like being punished for mistakes or missing something important—even when there was no real proof or official action to support that belief?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s closer to it.\n\nI’ll replay a shift over and over—like a camera angle I didn’t check, or a call I made late—and it starts to feel like proof I’m careless. Dangerous, even. And if something happens while I’m there… part of me feels like I should be punished for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs that others might not share—about things like your health, relationships, or other parts of your life—that you felt were definitely true, even if there wasn’t clear proof?\n\nPatient: Not really… no.\n\nI mean, I worry about things, but I can’t think of anything like that outside of work. Not about my health or relationships or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other strong beliefs, even if others didn’t agree, that were focused on a single theme—like guilt, your job performance, or something being wrong with you—that you couldn’t shake, even if there wasn’t clear proof?\n\nPatient: Yes… mostly guilt, I guess.\n\nIt gets stuck on work. Like if I missed a camera angle, or I was slow making a call, I keep going over it and over it. It starts to feel like… proof that I’m careless. Like I’m dangerous to have there.\n\nAnd if something does go wrong on my watch, part of me feels like I deserve whatever punishment comes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that you were a bad or dangerous person because of your work mistakes, even when others told you you were doing okay?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have.\n\nI’ll keep thinking about a missed camera angle or a call I made too late, and it starts feeling like that means something about me. Like I’m careless… maybe dangerous to have there. Even if someone says it was okay, I don’t really believe it.\n\nAnd if something goes wrong on my watch, I feel like I should be punished for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any delusional or strongly held beliefs that were not related to guilt, work performance, or being punished for mistakes?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so.\n\nIt’s mostly that one area. I can’t really think of anything outside of that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any strange or unusual beliefs that were not related to your work or feeling guilty about mistakes, even if others didn’t share them?\n\nPatient: Not really… no. I can’t think of anything like that outside of the work stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other types of strange sensations, voices, or beliefs that didn’t seem connected to your work or guilt about mistakes—like seeing things others don’t or believing something strongly that isn’t based in reality?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so.\n\nI mean, I haven’t noticed anything like that. It’s just… the stuff I already said. I don’t think there’s anything else."
